# Markdown Source Accessibility Baseline

This report assesses local Markdown source, not rendered pages or WCAG conformance. The accompanying `scores.jsonl` contains the article-level evidence and scores.

## 1 Average Scores

**Audit date:** 2026-11-02 (UTC). **Source commit:** `936049f38e0c137c87d60218e901290295fe494b`. **Result:** Complete source baseline (not a rendered accessibility certification).

| Inventory measure | Count |
|---|---:|
| Discovered Markdown files, including this report | 1535 |
| Included eligible articles | 1445 |
| Scored articles | 1445 |
| Excluded discovered Markdown files | 90 |
| Unscored / failed eligible articles | 0 |
| Reserved non-Markdown audit artifact (`scores.jsonl`; outside Markdown denominator) | 1 |
| Pruned directories (descendant file counts intentionally unknown) | 2 |

| Dimension | Weight | Mean (1–5) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Heading structure | 0.20 | 4.0 |
| Alternative text | 0.20 | 4.8 |
| Link text clarity | 0.15 | 4.9 |
| Plain language | 0.15 | 4.7 |
| Table accessibility | 0.10 | 3.2 |
| Formatting independence | 0.10 | 5.0 |
| Callout structure | 0.05 | 5.0 |
| Code block accessibility | 0.05 | 4.8 |

**Repository mean: 4.5 / 5.0.** Exact mean before display rounding: `4.513079584775086505190311419`. Total exact weighted numerator: `652140`; denominator: `144500`.

## 2 Summary

The strongest measured dimension is **formatting independence** (5.0); the weakest is **table accessibility** (3.2). This ranking includes absent-type scores of 5 and is a source-check ranking, not a complete semantic judgment.

| Confirmed severity | Finding occurrences | Unique articles |
|---|---:|---:|
| Critical | 0 | 0 |
| High | 666 | 618 |
| Critical or High (union, not sum) | 666 | 618 |

No Critical finding is asserted: source checks alone did not establish blockage of essential content. Headerless HTML environment/product/version tables dominate High findings; these are data label–value relationships, not failures to bold GFM header text.

Recurring confirmed Medium/Low patterns are counted below. An occurrence is one deduplicated rule/dimension/source-line finding. Multiple long sentences or blank cells on the same line collapse to one evidence occurrence, while scoring counters still use the explicitly defined sentence/cell thresholds.

| Pattern | Severity | Dimension | Occurrences | Unique articles |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| heading_skip | Medium | heading_structure | 684 | 684 |
| missing_h1 | Medium | heading_structure | 671 | 671 |
| untagged_fence | Medium | code_block_accessibility | 88 | 88 |
| empty_heading | Medium | heading_structure | 34 | 20 |
| empty_table_header | Medium | table_accessibility | 31 | 17 |
| bare_url | Medium | link_text_clarity | 20 | 19 |
| unclear_link | Medium | link_text_clarity | 13 | 13 |
| multiple_h1 | Medium | heading_structure | 4 | 4 |
| malformed_heading | Medium | heading_structure | 3 | 2 |
| long_sentence | Low | plain_language | 416 | 279 |
| code_no_leadin | Low | code_block_accessibility | 348 | 163 |
| empty_table_cell | Low | table_accessibility | 186 | 84 |
| complex_vocabulary | Low | plain_language | 108 | 95 |
| nonstandard_callout | Low | callout_structure | 3 | 3 |
| complex_table_no_leadin | Low | table_accessibility | 2 | 2 |

**Human Review Required:** 2070 cases in 566 unique articles. Unknown image purpose has no severity and is not included in the confirmed Medium/Low table. It affects score confidence, not scored/unscored status.

No browser, image inspection, remote snippet resolution, site build, historical PR 2303 review, or WCAG pass/fail claim is part of this assessment. It assesses the checked-out source at the commit above. Human review is necessary for image equivalence and purpose, embedded content, natural-language quality beyond the specified checks, and all rendered interaction/visual behavior.

## 3 Critical and High Issues

Findings are grouped by article in ascending path order, with Critical before High within each article. Line numbers are one-based original source lines, including frontmatter and blank lines. The **Issue** column quotes the original source line; source indentation is retained in JSONL. No per-article scores appear in this report.

| Severity | Article | Line | Dimension | Issue | Recommended Fix |

## Methodology and Scoring Rules

### Publication Scope and Normalization

The inventory recursively considers case-insensitive `.md` and `.markdown` extensions. It prunes `.git`, `.github`, `node_modules`, `_site`, `bin`, and `obj` directories without reading their contents. Descendant files in these pruned directories are outside the inventory; their unknown file counts are not reported as zero. The excluded-file inventory below covers every discovered Markdown file that was not scored.

The publication rules use `_config.yml` exclusions, Jekyll's underscore-prefixed source-directory convention, article frontmatter, and source context. `publish: false` and `published: false` are both excluded as requested. `_plugins/navigation_generator.rb:16–27` only filters navigation; `exclude_navigation`, `include_in_navigation: false`, and `sitemap: false` are **not** publication exclusions. `_plugins/sitemap.xml:4–10` confirms that sitemap suppression is separate. `docs-builder.yml` has navigation metadata and source replacements but no publication exclusion list. Its `code-snippets-basePath` is not resolved.

The `docs-builder.yml:375` “Hidden From TOC” section likewise controls navigation, not publication. Across all ten `hidden: true` navigation rules, 31 otherwise eligible articles remain included in this baseline.

`doc-output/` contains authored export documentation and remains in scope; its name is not evidence that it is generated output. Translation resource READMEs without article frontmatter, underscore-prefixed include/template/API material, explicitly unpublished files, and audit artifacts are excluded. Both new output paths are reserved audit artifacts; `scores.jsonl` is not Markdown and therefore is not added to the Markdown denominator.

Both `report-designer-translations/README.md:1–7` and `html5-report-viewer-translations/README.md:1–3` describe localization resource files and have no YAML frontmatter. They are resource READMEs, not published documentation articles; the first is also explicitly excluded by `_config.yml`. `_contentTemplates/common/parameters-table-styles.md:1–8` contains only the `#table-layout`/`#end` fragment markers and a style block, without article frontmatter. It is an unpublished snippet fragment, not a malformed article heading.

Excluding the new report, the source inventory reconciles exactly as follows: **1,534 − 84 explicitly unpublished − 2 `_assetsApi` files − 1 Report Designer translation README − 1 snippet fragment − 1 HTML5 viewer translation README = 1,445 eligible articles**. `README.md` is already in the 84 unpublished files and is not subtracted again for its overlapping configuration exclusion. The additional HTML5 translation README explains the difference from a provisional estimate of 1,446. All five source Markdown files without frontmatter are identified above or in the excluded-file inventory; no eligible article lacks frontmatter.

UTF-8 BOMs and CRLF line endings are normalized for parsing, without changing original line numbering. Frontmatter delimiters allow trailing spaces; YAML is parsed with PyYAML. Frontmatter is never scored as prose. Article failures would be listed as **Provisional**, retained in the inventory, and counted as unscored rather than silently omitted.

### Source Parser and Detection Coverage

The baseline uses Python, `markdown-it-py` 3.0.0 (CommonMark with HTML and the table rule enabled), PyYAML 6.0.1, and Python's `HTMLParser`. Tokens and source maps distinguish fenced and indented code, inline code, reference links/images, setext headings, table header delimiters, and actual prose. Jekyll slug destinations containing spaces are replaced with same-length parser-safe characters before parsing, while evidence always uses the unchanged original source. Reference definitions share the parser environment.

HTML is inspected only outside Markdown code. The HTML parser skips comments, scripts, styles, and code contents for prose analysis, and recognizes headings, links, images, tables, and preformatted examples. Complete inline HTML source, including anchor text, is inspected; tag names inside API link labels and dynamic Liquid labels require human review rather than an automatic empty-label finding. Markdown within HTML text is tokenized separately where possible. HTML table layout/data ambiguity is referred for human review rather than automatically declared a missing-header defect. Product/version/environment label–value tables are identifiable data tables: their label cells need semantic row headers even when they have only one row. All 44 initially ambiguous HTML tables were individually reviewed from their source rows; they contain environment/property data, not layout, and were confirmed as headerless data tables.

HTML table inspection also preserves the token order and text of `html_inline` fragments inside Markdown table cells. This detects the headerless font-attribute tables at `doc-output/configure-the-report-engine/fontlibrary-element.md:50` and `doc-output/configure-the-report-engine/privatefonts-element.md:65`. Caption markers create a boundary for subsequent table parsing while original line numbers remain unchanged, including tables immediately following a caption without a blank line. The Markdown outer tables and HTML inner tables are counted separately.

Liquid directives, snippet placeholders, and include references are recorded but never expanded, fetched, or followed. Math spans, URLs, code tokens, technical identifiers, and markup are removed from the prose sample. Heading children count as section content: an ordinary parent heading followed by child sections is not an empty section.

This is a deterministic source baseline, **not** a complete semantic human review. It does not determine rendered DOM semantics, CSS visibility, contrast, keyboard behavior, screen-reader announcements, external-link validity, whether a screenshot has an equivalent description, acronym familiarity, passive voice, true reading grade, or placeholder/result completeness. A score of 5 means no detected source defect in the assessed checks, not proven accessibility. Every article carries these coverage qualifications in its JSONL `assessment_scope`; unresolved local features also appear in `human_review_required`.

The plain-language sample covers running prose, including list and callout text, but excludes headings and table-cell bodies. Long explanations inside table cells therefore need a separate readability review. Lexical filtering removes code tokens, acronyms, and CamelCase identifiers; it is not a semantic classifier for every technical expression.

### Severity and Human Review

**Critical** is reserved for source-verifiable blockage of essential content. No automatic heuristic assigns Critical. **High** applies to repeated forward heading-level skips in one article, positively identified HTML data tables without semantic headers, and multiple untagged fenced blocks in one article. Each affected skip or fence is an occurrence; each table is an occurrence.

**Medium** covers isolated heading skips, missing or multiple H1s, empty or malformed headings, clearly informative images with absent or generic alternatives, non-descriptive links, empty table headers, explicit color-only instructions, and an isolated untagged fence. **Low** covers narrowly vague headings, long prose sentences, complex vocabulary, empty table data cells, complex tables without an adjacent prose lead-in, formatting-only signals, nonstandard/empty callouts, and code without an adjacent prose lead-in.

Spaced blockquote variants such as `> note After installing...` are valid source notes, not malformed callouts. The source plugin recognizes the compact typed markers, while a spaced variant remains a valid Markdown blockquote; this source-only baseline does not promise identical rendered styling. `>caption` is an image-caption marker, not a callout, and is excluded from callout applicability counts.

Unknown image purpose is **Human Review Required**, never Critical/High/Medium/Low. A filename or existing alt text alone does not establish informative purpose. Only adjacent prose explicitly referring to a screenshot, image, figure, chart, or diagram establishes informative context for the image checks. Explicit decorative attributes or an explicit adjacent decorative label allow empty alternatives. Uncertain alt equivalence is referred to human review even when the alt text is nonempty.

### Exact Scoring Thresholds

All dimension scores are integers from 1 through 5. The following rules are applied in order. Occurrence counts count findings, not source lines or articles, except where explicitly stated. No dimension is silently omitted from the weighted calculation.

| Dimension | Weight | Reproducible thresholds |
|---|---:|---|
| Heading structure | 0.20 | 1 if at least three H1s, at least three forward skips, or at least three empty headings; otherwise 2 if exactly two H1s or exactly two forward skips; otherwise 3 if any missing H1, isolated skip, empty heading, or malformed ATX heading; otherwise 4 if a narrowly vague heading is detected; otherwise 5. Headings are absent only when no Markdown or HTML heading exists; an article with none receives 5 by the requested absent-type rule and a human-review entry about the missing outline. A forward skip increases the previous level by more than one, with initial virtual level 0. A heading is empty only when its section has no nonblank content and no deeper child heading before the next equal/shallower heading. Malformed ATX candidates are lines outside code with one through six leading hash markers immediately followed by nonspace text, or seven or more markers followed by whitespace; they are evidence, not valid headings. Vague labels are exactly “details”, “more info”, “more information”, “misc”, “section”, or “section N”. |
| Alternative text | 0.20 | Consider only images whose informative purpose is established by adjacent prose. A deficient alternative is absent, empty, exactly “image”, “picture”, “screenshot”, “diagram”, “chart”, “figure”, “logo”, or a numbered image label, or a filename-like value. For n informative images and d deficient alternatives: 1 if d/n > 0.5; otherwise 2 if d >= 3; otherwise 3 if d is 1 or 2; otherwise 5. Any unresolved image purpose/equivalence caps the score at 4, without assigning severity to unknown purpose. No images gives 5. |
| Link text clarity | 0.15 | Defective labels are empty text-only links; exactly “here”, “click here”, “more”, “more info”, “more information”, “read more”, “this”, “this article”, “link”, or “learn more”; and a URL used as its own label or a bare prose URL. For n detected links/bare URLs and d defects: 1 if d/n > 0.5; otherwise 2 if d >= 3; otherwise 3 if d is 1 or 2; otherwise 5. Image-only links receive human review rather than an empty-label finding. Different API/member names or descriptive names for the same target are not defects. An unresolved dynamic/markup/image-only label caps the score at 4 without a severity finding. No links gives 5. |
| Plain language | 0.15 | Only prose sentences with at least four natural-language words are sampled. Long means more than 35 words; words exclude technical identifiers, inline code, URLs, markup, directives, and math. For n sampled sentences, l long sentences, and v occurrences of “utilize”, “utilizes”, “utilizing”, “aforementioned”, “henceforth”, or “heretofore”: 1 if l/n > 0.5 and n >= 4; otherwise 2 if l/n > 0.3 and n >= 4; otherwise 3 if l/n > 0.15 or l >= 3 or v >= 3; otherwise 4 if l > 0 or v > 0; otherwise 5. No sampled prose gives 5 and a review entry, not a claim that embedded content was assessed. |
| Table accessibility | 0.10 | 1 if any confirmed HTML data table lacks headers; otherwise 2 if any empty semantic header; otherwise 3 if at least three empty-data-cell or missing-complex-table-lead-in findings; otherwise 4 if one or two such findings or an ambiguous HTML table needs review; otherwise 5. Complex means at least five columns. Markdown delimiter rows are semantic headers and do not require bold text. No tables gives 5. |
| Formatting independence | 0.10 | 2 if an explicit color-only instruction occurs; otherwise 3 if at least three underline, all-bold warning, or manual em-dash pseudo-list signals occur; otherwise 4 if one or two such signals occur; otherwise 5. Color-only detection requires “red/green/blue/yellow items/rows/entries indicate/mean/require” without an adjacent explicit label/icon cue. Underline detection applies to HTML `<u>` outside links/code; all-bold warning detection requires a whole bold paragraph starting “Warning”, “Caution”, or “Important”. No candidate signals gives 5; visual reliance beyond these checks remains unassessed. |
| Callout structure | 0.05 | 2 if at least three malformed/empty callouts; otherwise 3 if two; otherwise 4 if one; otherwise 5. Typed `>tip`, `>note`, `>important`, `>caution`, and `>warning` are valid, as confirmed by `_plugins/alert_generator.rb:7–22`; plain blockquotes are valid notes. A malformed callout is a plain/bold “Note:”, “Tip:”, “Important:”, “Caution:”, or “Warning:” paragraph instead of platform markup. Empty typed callouts or ones containing only “See above/below.” are incomplete. No callouts gives 5. Severity appropriateness and overuse require human judgment. |
| Code block accessibility | 0.05 | For f fenced blocks and u missing language tags: 1 if u >= 2 and u/f > 0.5; otherwise 2 if u >= 2; otherwise 3 if u = 1; otherwise 4 if any code lacks an adjacent prose lead-in or an indented/HTML block has unresolved language metadata; otherwise 5. Missing language means the entire opening-fence info string is blank; named tabs/language aliases count as metadata. An adjacent lead-in is the immediately preceding nonblank prose line, with at least four natural words or a trailing colon, not a heading, code, or directive. No code gives 5; opaque snippets do not count as assessed code and are referred for review. |

Weights sum to 1.00. The exact article value is the integer weighted sum divided by 100, with weights `20,20,15,15,10,10,5,5` in the dimension order shown. `overall_score_unrounded` preserves that exact hundredth value; `overall_score` uses decimal half-up rounding to one decimal. Dimension means use integer dimension scores. The repository mean averages **unrounded** article values, not the displayed values. All displayed aggregate scores use the same decimal half-up rule.

JSONL records use the same schema throughout: relative `file`; `status`; `assessment_scope`; `feature_counts`; all eight `dimensions` with `score`, `applicable_count`, and evidence line numbers in `violations`; `overall_score`; `overall_score_unrounded`; `critical_high_findings`; `recurring_issues`; and `human_review_required`. Confirmed findings include severity, dimension, rule, one-based line, original source, and recommended fix. Human-review entries have no severity field. Unknown purpose does not by itself make an article unscored.

## Validation Results

The audit ran as direct `python - <<'PY'` commands using the already installed parsers; no helper script, dependency, test infrastructure, or rendered site was added. The audit generated only the two requested artifacts. Verification re-read the serialized JSONL and original source rather than relying solely on in-memory values.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| One-to-one inventory reconciliation | PASS: 1535 = 1445 scored + 90 excluded + 0 unscored; no duplicate article paths |
| JSONL parse and consistent schema | PASS: 1445 independently deserialized records; all eight dimensions and the same feature-counter keys present |
| Integer score bounds | PASS: 11560 scores are integers in 1–5 |
| Exact weighted math | PASS: 1445 article values recomputed from integer weights; rounded and unrounded values agree |
| Aggregate math | PASS: dimension sums and repository mean recomputed from unrounded weighted numerators |
| Absent-type rule | PASS: 5108 dimension instances have applicability 0 and score 5 |
| Source evidence | PASS: 5347 issue/review entries reference valid lines and match the original full source line |
| Confirmed Critical/High evidence | PASS: 666 findings have original source and a recommendation; repeated-fence/skip rules checked; all 618 headerless data tables rechecked for td cells and absence of th headers; initial 44 ambiguous contexts individually reviewed |
| Human-review severity separation | PASS: every human-review entry omits severity, including unknown-purpose images |
| Source callout/caption variants | PASS: no malformed-callout finding on 219 source lines with spaced typed markers or 69 caption-marker lines; captions excluded from callout counts |
| Both unpublished flags | PASS: every scored article independently rechecked for absence of publish: false and published: false |
| Independent source inventory | PASS: 1534 pre-audit Markdown files reconcile to 1445 eligible articles using 84 unpublished files and five additional excluded resource/fragment files |
| TOC-only hidden rules | PASS: all 31 otherwise eligible articles covered by the ten hidden navigation rules remain scored |
| Nested inline HTML tables | PASS: both source-identified font attribute tables classified as High; an independent all-article scan found no other missed inline/caption-adjacent tables |
| Source-only snippet handling | PASS: both affected articles have zero local code blocks and zero untagged fences; all five snippet directives remain unresolved human-review cases |
| Provisional / failed paths | None |
| Parser/rule edge cases | PASS: 20 assertions, listed below |

* GFM delimiters are headers without bold.
* Parent followed by child is not empty.
* Setext and reference link/image recognized.
* Unknown-purpose image has no severity.
* All five typed callouts are valid.
* Fenced/indented/inline code does not become prose markup.
* Repeated heading skips and multiple fences are High.
* One untagged fence is Medium.
* HTML label-value data lacks semantic headers.
* Explicit decorative image allowed.
* Different descriptive API link names are not ambiguity.
* Snippet is opaque and not silently assessed code.
* Decimal half-up rounding.
* Inline HTML preserves its actual anchor text.
* Liquid link label is not reduced to an empty label.
* Malformed ATX headings are source findings.
* HTML examples in code are not live links.
* Inline HTML data tables nested in Markdown cells preserve structure.
* Caption markers separate subsequent Markdown tables for tokenization.
* YAML delimiter whitespace and publication false.

Detected coverage: 8245 valid headings plus 3 malformed heading candidates; 960 images; 10561 links/bare URLs; 977 tables; 30560 sampled prose sentences; 1291 code blocks (1221 fenced, 69 indented); 1020 unresolved snippet/include/directive occurrences. Counters reflect detected source constructs, not rendered elements.

A small independent verification can be repeated from the repository root with the following command. The inventory exclusion policy and all scoring thresholds needed to interpret the resulting records are documented above; evidence is not a claim that every possible semantic defect was detected.

```python
import json
from decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_UP
from pathlib import Path
weights = dict(zip(
    ('heading_structure', 'alternative_text', 'link_text_clarity',
     'plain_language', 'table_accessibility', 'formatting_independence',
     'callout_structure', 'code_block_accessibility'),
    (20, 20, 15, 15, 10, 10, 5, 5)))
records = [json.loads(s) for s in Path('scores.jsonl').read_text().splitlines()]
assert len({r['file'] for r in records}) == len(records)
exact = []
for r in records:
    value = sum(Decimal(r['dimensions'][d]['score']) * w
                for d, w in weights.items()) / 100
    assert value == Decimal(str(r['overall_score_unrounded']))
    assert value.quantize(Decimal('0.1'), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP) == Decimal(str(r['overall_score']))
    exact.append(value)
    lines = Path(r['file']).read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig').splitlines()
    for key in ('critical_high_findings', 'recurring_issues', 'human_review_required'):
        for finding in r[key]:
            assert lines[finding['line'] - 1] == finding['source']
    for dimension in r['dimensions'].values():
        assert 1 <= dimension['score'] <= 5
        assert dimension['applicable_count'] or dimension['score'] == 5
    assert all('severity' not in h for h in r['human_review_required'])
print(len(records), sum(exact) / len(exact))
```

Run the code with `python - <<'PY'`, followed by the block and the closing `PY` delimiter. Final checks: `git diff --check` passed; `git status --short` showed only the two requested untracked artifact paths. No existing documentation, source, configuration, or helper script was changed.
